# Evidence ledger: fc_uk_mortgages_2026_09

- Question: Assume today is 2026-10-27. You know nothing published after this date. Estimate the number of net mortgage approvals for house purchase in the United Kingdom in September 2026 (seasonally adjusted), as it will be first reported in the Bank of England's monthly Money and Credit statistical release.
- as_of: 2026-10-27
- Pinned target: Number of net mortgage approvals for house purchase in the United Kingdom, seasonally adjusted, as first reported in the Bank of England's monthly Money and Credit statistical release for September 2026 (approvals net of cancellations, i.e. approvals minus cancellations within the month) | unit net mortgage approvals for house purchase | period 2026-09-01..2026-09-30 | publisher Bank of England (Money and Credit statistical release)

### d1 [own_history] BoE Money and Credit release archive

### d4 [related_series] Mortgage lenders and broker commentary

### d6 [physical_flow] Housing transaction pipeline

Hypothesis: Approvals convert to completions after a lag; HMRC residential transaction counts and RICS sales/agreed-sales indicators reflect the same underlying purchase flow and can constrain the approvals level via a stable approvals-to-completions ratio. Role: independent structural check.

Queries issued by the searcher: HMRC residential property transactions 2026 monthly; Bank of England money and credit September 2026 mortgage approvals; RICS residential market survey September 2026 new buyer enquiries agreed sales; HMRC property transactions August 2026 seasonally adjusted residential; Bank of England money and credit September 2026 mortgage approvals; HMRC monthly residential property transactions 2026; RICS residential market survey September 2026 agreed sales new buyer enquiries; UK net mortgage approvals house purchase 2026; HMRC monthly residential property transactions 2026; Bank of England money and credit mortgage approvals August 2026; RICS residential market survey September 2026 agreed sales new buyer enquiries; HMRC residential property transactions August 2026 provisional seasonally adjusted 30 September 2026

- m1 coverage: the whole target
- m2 coverage: the whole target
- m3 coverage: the whole target

Method details (computed by code):

- m1 formula_mc: p16 * a1; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 37,416 to 81,861; result unit (approval/month) * 1 month converted to approval; own 80% width x/÷1.48 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m2 formula_mc: p1 * a2; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 44,437 to 67,980; result unit (approval/month) * 1 month converted to approval; own 80% width x/÷1.38 (no error evidence, code's default width)
- m3 formula_mc: p110; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 45,252 to 65,177; result unit (approval/month) * 1 month converted to approval; own 80% width x/÷1.38 (no error evidence, code's default width)
- Combination: 3 block(s), equal weight per block (methods whose inputs overlap by at least half form one block; backtest errors set widths, not weights): B1 = m1: 55,083 (width x/÷1.48, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B2 = m2: 54,766 (width x/÷1.38, no error evidence, code's default width); B3 = m3: 54,151 (width x/÷1.38, no error evidence, code's default width). Estimate = the median of the blocks.
- Interval: root mean square of the block widths x/÷1.41; Birge ratio 0.03 (not above one, so no widening); final width x/÷1.41.

Result: central **54,766** approval, 80% interval [38,759, 77,384] approval, basis blocks (several routes with separate inputs: the width is the typical width of the routes' own intervals, not narrowed for their number), weight rule equal_blocks_median, weight on methods that use assumptions 1.00

Unit of the estimate: approval (net mortgage approvals for house purchase; total for 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-30)

## Final answer

The best estimate is 54,766 approval (80% model-based interval 38,759 to 77,384 approval) for net mortgage approvals for house purchase in the United Kingdom, seasonally adjusted, September 2026, as first reported in the Bank of England Money and Credit release.

Main evidence: the August 2026 print of 54,900 net mortgage approvals for house purchase anchors a near-flat month-on-month bridge (factor 1, range 0.8933 to 1.117), supported by the September consensus of 54,300 mortgage approvals for house purchase and modest positive search/valuation signals. A separate route scales the September 2025 print of 65,900 net mortgage approvals for house purchase by a year-on-year ratio of 0.84 (range 0.5925 to 1.185).

Observed: the August and September 2025 prints and the consensus. Assumed: both bridge factors. Code combined three blocks by equal weight per block, taking the median; the interval reflects the blocks' own widths.

Key risk: the consensus overshot August, so the flat bridge may be biased high. Better evidence would be early September approvals or timely lender data.

Unit of the estimate: approval (net mortgage approvals for house purchase; total for 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-30).

Key assumptions (proposed by the analyst model, not observed; code samples each one between the bounds shown):
- a1 September 2026 to September 2025 approvals ratio: 0.84 [n], range 0.5925 to 1.185 (range widened by code); used by m1. Overturned by: Evidence of a sharp September rebound (e.g. purchase searches up strongly y/y) would push the ratio toward [n].
- a2 September 2026 to August 2026 approvals change factor: 1 [n], range 0.8933 to 1.117 (range widened by code); used by m2. Overturned by: A large September search or valuation surge/drop would move the factor outside the range.

Method details (computed by code):
- m1 formula_mc: p16 * a1; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 37,416 to 81,861; result unit (approval/month) * 1 month converted to approval; own 80% width x/÷1.48 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m2 formula_mc: p1 * a2; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 44,437 to 67,980; result unit (approval/month) * 1 month converted to approval; own 80% width x/÷1.38 (no error evidence, code's default width)
- m3 formula_mc: p110; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 45,252 to 65,177; result unit (approval/month) * 1 month converted to approval; own 80% width x/÷1.38 (no error evidence, code's default width)
- Combination: 3 block(s), equal weight per block (methods whose inputs overlap by at least half form one block; backtest errors set widths, not weights): B1 = m1: 55,083 (width x/÷1.48, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B2 = m2: 54,766 (width x/÷1.38, no error evidence, code's default width); B3 = m3: 54,151 (width x/÷1.38, no error evidence, code's default width). Estimate = the median of the blocks.
- Interval: root mean square of the block widths x/÷1.41; Birge ratio 0.03 (not above one, so no widening); final width x/÷1.41.
